- 2026-10-28 09:23 · 005 evidence · A second field report (2026-10-27): decide without --by records the ruling as the owner's, so a coordinator's provisional ruling went in under the owner's name by omission, and the text cannot be corrected. This issue covers that too: a ruling without --by is the caller's (the coordinator's), never the owner's by default; the owner's needs --by and the quote.
- 2026-10-28 09:23 · 003 evidence · A second field report (2026-10-27): from round 3 on, every round printed the takeover block with the whole record, about 12,000 characters each time, while the rounds converged (29, 14, 3, 3, 1 findings). This issue covers that too: the block once per takeover and a pointer to show after that; and a round may record its count of findings, so a converging issue is told apart from a failing one.

- 2026-11-03 09:03 · asked a-004 (stuck) · Does 6.3.0 change how Jarl writes its record, once, with Horde re-vendoring Jarl in the same release? Context: the model's owner-only acceptance mark, one record for one review of several issues, a collision-proof key per issue so two clones can be repaired (technical critique I5), and the writer named on a write all change what the log or the issue file carries, so the record format rises; Horde vendors Jarl v6.1.0 (format 1 only) and already cannot read a loop the current Jarl starts (technical critique I10). Options (labels, not the owner's wording): (1) one format raise in 6.3.0 for every such change, the jarl-record/2 contract, and a Horde issue in this release to re-vendor Jarl, so the release is no longer Jarl alone; (2) no format change in 6.3.0: only what derives from what is already written (the list of what is on whom, briefs from the record, rules with a scope if they fit), and the mark, the shared-review record, the per-issue key and the writer wait for a later release. Recommended: (1).

- 2026-11-03 09:05 · asked a-005 (stuck) · Two machines on one loop: is 'caught and repaired at the next merge' acceptable in place of 'collide on nothing' (ruling loop-tool-one-model-five-scenarios)? Context: with the format raise of one-format-raise-horde-later, every issue gets a collision-proof key, so two issues that took one number in two clones are told apart and renumbered at the merge, and numbers are taken over every branch the clone knows; but the same issue leased in two clones before either pushes is only seen after the merge, because Jarl never pushes or fetches by itself (technical critique I4, I5). Options (labels, not the owner's wording): (1) caught at the merge: the record is never mixed, a doubled number is renumbered, a double lease shows to the jarl as a conflict, and the coordinator's brief says to pull before leasing and push right after, so a double lease is rare but possible; (2) prevented: a lease counts only once it is pushed, the coordinator's list shows a lease not yet on the remote and refuses to treat it as held, which needs a reachable remote for every lease. Recommended: (1).
- 2026-11-03 09:07 · answered a-005 · Krzysztof, 2026-11-03: "1": option (1), caught at the merge (option label, not his wording). The full ruling is two-machines-caught-at-merge.

- 2026-11-03 10:16 · 041 evidence · Ruling approval-defined-by-the-loop: Krzysztof, 2026-11-03 (translated from Polish): "1, but it also depends on the loop: whether we iterate locally or on pull requests (to a feature branch, for example). There, other bots may come in, like CodeRabbit or Copilot. As a rule I work only in local mode. The loop should define what an approved issue means." Each loop defines what an approved issue is: which reviewers' approves clear it (a fresh agent, the owner, a bot the loop names) and what must come with them. With no definition, option (1) of a-011 holds: a bot's verdicts are recorded and counted apart, its findings are welcome, and only a fresh agent's or the owner's approve clears. Built today: a profile's done gate already says approve fresh or none, requires-merged and required slots; the definition extends that.

- 2026-11-03 11:38 · 019 evidence · Ruling retracted-note-reason-hidden-from-workers: Decided by the analysis session (design of research/2026-11-03-jarl-as-a-loop-tool), asked by 019's worker: what a worker reads (show, and later the brief of 058) hides a retracted note and the reason of its retraction alike, and says only how many notes were retracted, so an absence is never silent; the reason stays in the log and in the full history, where the coordinator and reviewers read it. A reason often restates the claim it retracts, and a correction that a worker must follow is written as a new note, not as a reason. A note's retraction reuses the shape of a row's retraction and rides the single format raise of 083.

- 2026-11-04 18:18 · asked a-014 (stuck) · Issue 096, after six reviews: should a refused call end with a line that can be run as printed, or always with the command's form with a placeholder to fill in? Today a refusal can print a corrected call that runs (a value in the wrong case fixed, a removed command such as prio or body replaced by field). Six rounds of review kept finding that such a line, pasted, does something slightly other than the call meant: a word lost, a flag with its value dropped, a second call that runs after the first was refused. Options: (1) keep runnable lines and fix this round's findings, with another review round; (2) no refusal ends with a runnable corrected call: every refusal says what is wrong and ends with the command's form and a placeholder (a removed command ends with the field form that replaces it), which removes the whole class and costs the agent one step to fill the value in; (3) keep runnable lines only for the removed commands with the plainest forms (prio, tag with one change) and the placeholder form for everything else. Recommendation: (2).
- 2026-11-04 19:12 · decided refusals-end-in-a-form-not-a-guess · by jarl
- 2026-11-04 19:12 · 096 evidence · Ruling refusals-end-in-a-form-not-a-guess: Under autonomy-quality, at the owner's word 'do it so that it is good' and his question 'which is better in quality' (2026-11-04, translated from Polish; he did not choose an option): no refusal of Jarl ends with a corrected call the tool composed for the caller. Every refusal says what is wrong, in a reason that names the caller's wrong word, and ends with the form of the command that does what was meant, with a one-token placeholder from the closed registry for the value only the caller knows; a removed command ends with the form of the command that replaces it (field <ids> Priority <value>). Pasted unfilled, such a line is refused for its placeholder and writes nothing. Why: six reviews of issue 096 each found one more case in which a composed runnable line, pasted, did something other than the call meant (a word lost, a flag with its value dropped, a second call that ran after the first was refused); the class is removed, not another instance of it. Ready commands that carry no guess (a step the caller must take, shown as its own command) stay as commands. The acceptance line 'ends with a ready command or the flag to add' is met by the form with its placeholder. a-014 stays open for the owner's confirmation.

- 2026-11-04 21:46 · decided host-rules-place-worktrees-and-scratch · by jarl
- 2026-11-04 21:53 · asked a-015 (stop) · The host's rules put working copies under ~/Worktrees/<Repository>/<name>. Jarl writes a lease's worktree path into the loop record, and Althing's private-paths rail (rails/paths.mjs, ALLOWED_WORKTREE) allows that only under ~/.jarl-wt, so the loop cannot commit a lease (issue 096's header and log line 1208 are refused today). The fix is one narrow change to that regex (issue 143, written and tested in the worktree jarl/143-host-worktree-place, 100 tests green, not committed). The gate will not take it without your admission: rails/paths.mjs is the code of two rules that reach whole node types, boundary/no-private-paths and links/resolve, so editing it voids your ratification of 2026-10-06 and each must be admitted again with yg log add --aspect <rule> --ratify. Options: (1) admit the narrow change and re-ratify the two rules (recommended); (2) leave the rail alone, record new leases with no worktree path and rewrite the two existing lines of the 096 lease by hand (a loss of the worktree in the record, and a hand edit of the log); (3) make the two rules advisory until you can look at them.

- 2026-11-05 11:50 · 098 evidence · Ruling outside-finding-found-by-reviewer-where-names-the-review: Under autonomy-quality, where the acceptance line of issue 098 and its own Changelog line disagree (code-docs-conflicts: an obvious reading, no real choice of behaviour): the acceptance says each finding filed outside the issue is a new issue 'whose finder is the reviewed issue'; the Changelog line says 'found by that reviewer'; this loop's every issue has Found by naming the reviewer and Where naming the review of the reviewed issue. A new issue filed by review --outside therefore has Found by = the reviewer's name and Where = 'the review of <reviewed id>', and the acceptance line is read that way: the finding traces to the reviewed issue through Where. Not a choice that is the owner's.

- 2026-11-08 07:55 · 157 evidence · Takeover, ae94c29: the field-by-field pairing is replaced by one rule. Every review line of the issue, approves and retractions too, and every Reviews entry take part. A pair is one review when one check finds the verdict, the reviewer, the kind the line records, the model, the relay mark, the batch and the text the same, and the day where both have one. An entry is a line's when every pairing with the most pairs pairs the two. The pairings keep the log's order and the file's while one of them places every entry a line fits, read from the most pairs before and after each place, so the cost is the product of the two lengths. Otherwise the file holds its entries out of the log's order or more of them than the lines they fit (a merge resolved by hand), the pairings are those in any order, and a new note says the place told nothing. Either way an entry a line fits goes unpaired only when another line or entry of its reviewer fits as well, so the note that nothing tells them apart is true wherever it is printed. The brief tests hold an oracle that enumerates the histories and every pairing of each one by one.
